The situation:

1. **Object eval**: Still all FAIL even with LoRA loaded and off-by-one fixed. The new eval (PID 398822) is showing FAIL at Ep 43/50, still no OK. This is very concerning. The off-by-one fix should have helped, but it didn't. The fix might not have been picked up by the tmux session that started before I checked. Wait - I fixed the script, then killed PID 388391, then restarted with submit_training_job. The new PID is 398822. The fix should be in effect.
   
   Looking at the log timing more carefully, the episodes are progressing at the right pace for a fresh run, but there are null bytes scattered throughout the log file, which suggests the old truncated output is still mixed in. Since the tmux session reuses the same name, the log is being appended to the existing file rather than starting fresh.

The real question is whether this is the new run with the off-by-one fix or still the old one. I need to check the model loading timestamps - the new run should have started after the fix was applied around 10:49, so the loading messages should appear after that time. But the log shows loading at 10:45, which would be from an earlier run, so I'm trying to figure out which version is actually running now.

When you resubmit with the same experiment ID, it kills the old tmux session and creates a new one, which overwrites the log file. Looking at the tail output showing episodes 43-44 around 10:51 with roughly 26-second gaps between them, that timing would be consistent with a run that started around 10:50 and has been progressing for about a minute.

But here's the problem: even the second run with LoRA loaded (before the off-by-one fix) was showing all failures, which suggests the core issue isn't just the off-by-one error.

I need to tackle the download lock problem first—I'll kill all the Hugging Face download processes, clear out the lock and incomplete files, and restart the downloads fresh. Then I should compare my Object evaluation setup against the official OFT code to see if there are other differences I'm missing. Once the downloads are unblocked and the third run starts producing results with both fixes applied, I'll have a clearer picture of what's actually broken.

This IS the third run (starting at 10:51:13). LoRA loaded at 10:52:16. The model is running with both fixes.

I see the peft warning: "Input and output embeddings are no longer tied after merging. Setting `tie_word_embeddings=False`". This is important - after LoRA merge, the embeddings and lm_head are untied. This means the lm_head has its own copy of weights now, which should be correct (the LoRA was applied to lm_head).

But the eval is STILL getting 0% success. 

The LoRA loading and off-by-one aren't the culprits here—there's something more fundamental wrong with how I'm running inference. I need to dig into the official OFT eval code and compare it against mine more carefully. The image preprocessing might be the issue—I'm doing a 180-degree rotation with `img[::-1, ::-1]` when the standard LIBERO approach just does a vertical flip. Or it could be how I'm constructing the multimodal input with the vision tokens, prompt, and action tokens arranged together. The action unnormalization formula using q99/q01 could also be off.

Looking at the official code, it uses `get_vla_action()` from `openvla_utils.py` which probably handles things differently than my custom `predict_actions_oft` function. The image flip is my best guess right now—if the model was trained with a specific transformation and I'm applying a different one, the spatial understanding would be completely broken. Let me verify whether OFT's training actually uses that 180-degree rotation or if it's just a vertical flip like standard LIBERO. I'm looking at the LIBERO evaluation code in the OFT repository to understand the exact image preprocessing pipeline they use—it's likely either a simple vertical flip or a 180-degree rotation of the agent view image. The critical issue is whether my `predict_actions_oft` function correctly implements the OFT inference pipeline, since the official approach uses the model's built-in `get_vla_action()` method while I'm manually constructing the multimodal input with vision tokens, prompt tokens, and action placeholders, which could introduce bugs. Let me reconsider the off-by-one fix in the context of how OFT handles action placeholder tokens in the autoregressive model. the OFT forward pass:
- Input: [BOS, &lt;image_patches>, prompt_tokens, action_placeholders]
- The LLM processes this and outputs hidden states at each position
- The hidden states at action_placeholder positions are fed to the action head

But in the standard causal LM, the hidden state at position i is computed from all tokens up to and including position i. The hidden state at the first action_placeholder sees all the image, prompt, and itself (zero). 

The action head expects the hidden states from the action_placeholder positions, not the prompt position. So the extraction should be at:
- Start: 1 (BOS) + n_patches + n_prompt (prompt tokens including trailing 29871)
- End: start + 56

With my fix: `a_start = 1 + n_patches + n_prompt`

Now I'm working through the actual token counts. If the tokenized prompt with BOS is `[1, tok_1, ..., tok_n, 29871]`, then `n_prompt = input_ids.shape[-1] - 1`. After extending with 56 zero embeddings, the multimodal sequence becomes `[embed(BOS), projected_vision(x n_patches), embed(tok_1), ..., embed(29871), zero_embeds(x56)]` with a total length of 1 + n_patches + n_prompt + 56.

The action placeholders start at position 1 + n_patches + n_prompt, which matches my fix. The embedding construction zeros out the last 56 positions of the extended input to ensure the action positions are truly zero vectors rather than the learned embedding of token 0. Then the multimodal sequence concatenates the vision embeddings with the text embeddings...

Continuing with the forward pass... I'm realizing the issue might be that I'm manually constructing the multimodal input differently from how the model was actually trained. The official OFT model uses its built-in forward method which handles image placeholder token replacement, but I'm doing this manually in my eval code. The critical difference could be in how the vision features are positioned relative to the BOS token and the rest of the prompt—the model might expect a different arrangement of these components than what I'm currently creating. OS tokens, the position of vision features relative to prompt tokens, etc.

Let me check: when the processor tokenizes the prompt with an image, how does it arrange the tokens?

Actually, looking at the OpenVLA architecture, the model uses a custom processor (`PrismaticProcessor`) that tokenizes the text prompt, processes the image into pixel values, and internally replaces the `&lt;image>` placeholder in the text with vision features.

But in my eval code, I'm NOT using the model's built-in replacement mechanism. Instead, I'm manually constructing the input by getting text embeddings and vision features separately, then concatenating them as [BOS | vision | rest_of_text | action_placeholders]. This manual construction might not match how the model was trained—it could expect vision features at the image placeholder position in the middle of the text, or follow the standard [system_prompt | image | user_prompt] format.

This could be a fundamental issue with the eval script! Let me check the OFT architecture to understand how it processes inputs. The model's `forward()` method in `modeling_prismatic.py` likely handles the vision-language fusion internally, but my eval code bypasses this by directly calling the vision backbone, projector, and language model separately. This should be fine if my manual construction matches the model's forward pass, but if the model does something extra during its forward pass, I might be missing crucial processing steps.

### Assistant

_source line 343_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I can see the key insight. The `predict_action` method in the model uses `self.generate()` which is the **autoregressive token generation** approach — it generates action tokens one by one using the vocabulary. This is the **original OpenVLA** approach (discretized into 256 bins).

But **OpenVLA-OFT** uses a completely different action prediction mechanism: it uses an **L1 regression action head** that takes hidden states from the last layer and predicts continuous actions directly. It does NOT use token generation.

So the `predict_action` method in the model code is for the **base OpenVLA**, not for OFT. The OFT approach:


1. Passes input through the model with `output_hidden_states=True`
2. Extracts hidden states at action token positions
3. Feeds those hidden states through `action_head` to get continuous actions
4. Uses action chunking (K=8 chunks)

Now I need to look at the eval script's `predict_actions_oft` function to understand how it implements this pipeline, and check whether there's an OFT-specific predict method in the model itself or if the eval script's manual approach is the standard way to do inference.

Let me examine what the `action_head` and `proprio_projector` components actually look like in the code.
```

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"        wrist_inputs = processor(prompt, wrist_pil).to(device, dtype=torch.bfloat16)\\n        patches_wrist = model.vision_backbone(wrist_inputs[\\\"pixel_values\\\"])\\n        all_patches = torch.cat([patches_primary, patches_wrist], dim=1)\\n    else:\\n        all_patches = patches_primary\\n\\n    projected = model.projector(all_patches)\\n\\n    # Proprio token\\n    if use_proprio and proprio_proj is not None and proprio_normed is not None:\\n        proprio_t = torch.tensor(\\n            proprio_normed, dtype=projected.dtype, device=device\\n        )\\n        proprio_emb = proprio_proj(proprio_t.unsqueeze(0)).unsqueeze(1)\\n        projected = torch.cat([projected, proprio_emb], dim=1)\\n\\n    n_patches = projected.shape[1]\\n\\n    # Multimodal sequence: [first_token | patches | rest_tokens]\\n    multimodal = torch.cat(\\n        [input_embeds[:, :1, :], projected, input_embeds[:, 1:, :]],\\n        dim=1,\\n    )\\n    patch_attn = torch.ones(1, n_patches, device=device, dtype=attn_ext.dtype)\\n    multimodal_attn = torch.cat(\\n        [attn_ext[:, :1], patch_attn, attn_ext[:, 1:]],\\n        dim=1,\\n    )\\n\\n    # LLM forward\\n    outputs = model.language_model(\\n        inputs_embeds=multimodal,\\n        attention_mask=multimodal_attn,\\n        output_hidden_states=True,\\n        return_dict=True,\\n    )\\n\\n    # Extract action hidden states\\n    last_h = outputs.hidden_states[-1]\\n    a_start = 1 + n_patches + n_prompt\\n    a_end = a_start + n_act_tokens\\n    action_h = last_h[:, a_start:a_end, :]\\n\\n    # Action head -> continuous actions\\n    pred = action_head.predict_action(action_h)\\n    pred = pred.reshape(NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK, ACTION_DIM).float().cpu().numpy()\\n\\n    # Unnormalize (bounds_q99)\\n    stats = model.norm_stats[unnorm_key][\\\"action\\\"]\\n    mask = np.array(stats.get(\\\"mask\\\", np.ones(ACTION_DIM, dtype=bool)))\\n    q99 = np.array(stats[\\\"q99\\\"], dtype=np.float32)\\n    q01 = np.array(stats[\\\"q01\\\"], dtype=np.float32)\\n    actions = np.where(\\n        mask,\\n        0.5 * (pred + 1) * (q99 - q01 + 1e-8) + q01,\\n        pred,\\n    )\\n\\n    return actions\\n\\n\\n# --- Main Evaluation ---\\n\\ndef run_evaluation(args):\\n    device = torch.device(f\\\"cuda:{args.gpu}\\\" if torch.cuda.is_available() else \\\"cpu\\\")\\n    max_steps = args.max_steps or SUITE_MAX_STEPS.get(args.suite, 300)\\n    output_path = Path(\\n        args.output\\n        or f\\\"eval_results/oft_prune_{args.prune_mode}_k{args.keep_ratio}_{args.suite}.json\\\"\\n    )\\n\\n    log.info(\\n        \\\"Suite=%s | Device=%s | prune=%s keep=%.2f | Ep/task=%d | max_steps=%d\\\",\\n        args.suite, device, args.prune_mode, args.keep_ratio, args.num_episodes, max_steps,\\n    )\\n\\n    np.random.seed(args.seed)\\n    torch.manual_seed(args.seed)\\n\\n    model, processor, action_head, proprio_proj = load_model_and_components(\\n        args.checkpoint, device, args.load_in_8bit, args.load_in_4bit,\\n    )\\n\\n    # ### Assistant

_source line 361_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The `[::-1, ::-1]` reverses both axes - first axis is height (vertical flip) and second is width (horizontal flip), which combined gives a 180-degree rotation. Let me check how the official OpenVLA-OFT code handles LIBERO images.

Let me also check the official OpenVLA-OFT repo's evaluation code. The question is: does the OFT paper use `[::-1, ::-1]` or `[::-1]` for LIBERO image extraction?

Actually, looking at this more carefully, for LIBERO environment images:


- `obs["agentview_image"]` returns an image in format (H, W, C) with (0,0) at bottom-left
- `[::-1]` would flip the first axis (vertical flip, correcting bottom-to-top), making (0,0) top-left
- `[::-1, ::-1]` would flip both axes: vertical flip AND horizontal flip = 180° rotation

Standard LIBERO convention uses just `[::-1]` for vertical flip. Using `[::-1, ::-1]` adds an unnecessary horizontal flip that would mirror the image left-right. Let me verify what MuJoCo/LIBERO actually does...
```

</details>

Start evaluation\\n    total_episodes, total_successes = 0, 0\\n    for task_id in tqdm.tqdm(range(num_tasks)):\\n        total_episodes, total_successes = run_task(\\n            cfg,\\n            task_suite,\\n            task_id,\\n            model,\\n            resize_size,\\n            processor,\\n            action_head,\\n            proprio_projector,\\n            noisy_action_projector,\\n            total_episodes,\\n            total_successes,\\n            log_file,\\n        )\\n\\n    # Calculate final success rate\\n    final_success_rate = float(total_successes) / float(total_episodes) if total_episodes > 0 else 0\\n\\n    # Log final results\\n    log_message(\\\"Final results:\\\", log_file)\\n    log_message(f\\\"Total episodes: {total_episodes}\\\", log_file)\\n    log_message(f\\\"Total successes: {total_successes}\\\", log_file)\\n    log_message(f\\\"Overall success rate: {final_success_rate:.4f} ({final_success_rate * 100:.1f}%)\\\", log_file)\\n\\n    # Log to wandb if enabled\\n    if cfg.use_wandb:\\n        wandb.log(\\n            {\\n                \\\"success_rate/total\\\": final_success_rate,\\n                \\\"num_episodes/total\\\": total_episodes,\\n            }\\n        )\\n        wandb.save(local_log_filepath)\\n\\n    # Close log file\\n    if log_file:\\n        log_file.close()\\n\\n    return final_success_rate\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    eval_libero()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.5}}"}

IMPORTANT: seed seems to affect object positions even when using fixed initial state\\n    return env, task_description\\n\\n\\ndef get_libero_dummy_action(model_family: str):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Get dummy/no-op action, used to roll out the simulation while the robot does nothing.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    return [0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, -1]\\n\\n\\ndef get_libero_image(obs):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Extracts third-person image from observations and preprocesses it.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    img = obs[\\\"agentview_image\\\"]\\n    img = img[::-1, ::-1]  # IMPORTANT: rotate 180 degrees to match train preprocessing\\n    return img\\n\\n\\ndef save_rollout_video(rollout_images, idx, success, task_description, log_file=None):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Saves an MP4 replay of an episode.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    rollout_dir = f\\\"./rollouts/{DATE}\\\"\\n    os.makedirs(rollout_dir, exist_ok=True)\\n    processed_task_description = task_description.lower().replace(\\\" \\\", \\\"_\\\").replace(\\\"\\\\n\\\", \\\"_\\\").replace(\\\".\\\", \\\"_\\\")[:50]\\n    mp4_path = f\\\"{rollout_dir}/{DATE_TIME}--openvla_oft--episode={idx}--success={success}--task={processed_task_description}.mp4\\\"\\n    video_writer = imageio.get_writer(mp4_path, fps=30)\\n    for img in rollout_images:\\n        video_writer.append_data(img)\\n    video_writer.close()\\n    print(f\\\"Saved rollout MP4 at path {mp4_path}\\\")\\n    if log_file is not None:\\n        log_file.write(f\\\"Saved rollout MP4 at path {mp4_path}\\\\n\\\")\\n    return mp4_path\\n\\n\\ndef quat2axisangle(quat):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Copied from robosuite: https://github.com/ARISE-Initiative/robosuite/blob/eafb81f54ffc104f905ee48a16bb15f059176ad3/robosuite/utils/transform_utils.py#L490C1-L512C55\\n\\n    Converts quaternion to axis-angle format.\\n    Returns a unit vector direction scaled by its angle in radians.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        quat (np.array): (x,y,z,w) vec4 float angles\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        np.array: (ax,ay,az) axis-angle exponential coordinates\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # clip quaternion\\n    if quat[3] > 1.0:\\n        quat[3] = 1.0\\n    elif quat[3] < -1.0:\\n        quat[3] = -1.0\\n\\n    den = np.sqrt(1.0 - quat[3] * quat[3])\\n    if math.isclose(den, 0.0):\\n        # This is (close to) a zero degree rotation, immediately return\\n        return np.zeros(3)\\n\\n    return (quat[:3] * 2.0 * math.acos(quat[3])) / den\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}

Note that unlike the other action\\n    dimensions, the gripper action is not normalized to [-1,+1] by default.\\n\\n    Normalization formula: y = 2 * (x - orig_low) / (orig_high - orig_low) - 1\\n\\n    Args:\\n        action: Action array with gripper action in the last dimension\\n        binarize: Whether to binarize gripper action to -1 or +1\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        np.ndarray: Action array with normalized gripper action\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Create a copy to avoid modifying the original\\n    normalized_action = action.copy()\\n\\n    # Normalize the last action dimension to [-1,+1]\\n    orig_low, orig_high = 0.0, 1.0\\n    normalized_action[..., -1] = 2 * (normalized_action[..., -1] - orig_low) / (orig_high - orig_low) - 1\\n\\n    if binarize:\\n        # Binarize to -1 or +1\\n        normalized_action[..., -1] = np.sign(normalized_action[..., -1])\\n\\n    return normalized_action\\n\\n\\ndef invert_gripper_action(action: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Flip the sign of the gripper action (last dimension of action vector).\\n\\n    This is necessary for environments where -1 = open, +1 = close, since\\n    the RLDS dataloader aligns gripper actions such that 0 = close, 1 = open.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        action: Action array with gripper action in the last dimension\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        np.ndarray: Action array with inverted gripper action\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Create a copy to avoid modifying the original\\n    inverted_action = action.copy()\\n\\n    # Invert the gripper action\\n    inverted_action[..., -1] *= -1.0\\n\\n    return inverted_action\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.52}}"

So the official eval's `predict_action` method handles action heads and proprio directly, whereas our eval script has to manually construct that pipeline since we're working without the official codebase's implementation.

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"    def _prepare_input_for_action_prediction(self, input_ids, attention_mask):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Prepares input for action prediction by adding necessary tokens\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        # Add (ACTION_DIM * NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK) placeholder tokens to input_ids to simulate action tokens\\n        placeholder_action_token_ids = (\\n            torch.ones((input_ids.shape[0], ACTION_DIM * NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK)).to(input_ids.device).to(input_ids.dtype)\\n        )\\n        input_ids = torch.cat([input_ids, placeholder_action_token_ids], dim=-1)\\n\\n        # Add stop token to sequence (needed in non-causal bi-directional self-attention, as it appears at train time)\\n        stop_token_id = torch.ones((input_ids.shape[0], 1)).to(input_ids.device).to(input_ids.dtype) * STOP_INDEX\\n        input_ids = torch.cat([input_ids, stop_token_id], dim=-1)\\n\\n        # Extend the attention mask to fit the new shape of input\\n        # Note: Only batch size == 1 supported right now\\n        mask_extension = (\\n            torch.ones((attention_mask.shape[0], input_ids.shape[-1] - attention_mask.shape[-1]))\\n            .to(attention_mask.device)\\n            .to(attention_mask.dtype)\\n        )\\n        attention_mask = torch.cat([attention_mask, mask_extension], dim=-1)\\n\\n        return input_ids, attention_mask\\n\\n    def _prepare_labels_for_action_prediction(self, labels, input_ids):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Creates labels tensor for action prediction if not provided\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        # Extend labels tensor with fake action labels\\n        ARBITRARY_ACTION_TOKEN_IDX = ACTION_TOKEN_BEGIN_IDX + 1\\n        labels_extension = (\\n            torch.ones((labels.shape[0], input_ids.shape[-1] - labels.shape[-1])).to(labels.device).to(labels.dtype)\\n            * ARBITRARY_ACTION_TOKEN_IDX\\n        )\\n        labels = torch.cat([labels, labels_extension], dim=-1)\\n\\n        # Replace last label token with stop token\\n        labels[:, -1] = STOP_INDEX\\n\\n        return labels\\n\\n    def _unnormalize_actions(self, normalized_actions, unnorm_key=None):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Unnormalize actions using dataset statistics\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        action_norm_stats = self.get_action_stats(unnorm_key)\\n\\n        if ACTION_PROPRIO_NORMALIZATION_TYPE == NormalizationType.BOUNDS:\\n            mask = action_norm_stats.get(\\\"mask\\\", np.ones_like(action_norm_stats[\\\"min\\\"], dtype=bool))\\n            action_high, action_low = np.array(action_norm_stats[\\\"max\\\"]), np.array(action_norm_stats[\\\"min\\\"])\\n        elif ACTION_PROPRIO_NORMALIZATION_TYPE == NormalizationType.BOUNDS_Q99:\\n            mask = action_norm_stats.get(\\\"mask\\\", np.ones_like(action_norm_stats[\\\"q01\\\"], dtype=bool))\\n            action_high, action_low = np.array(action_norm_stats[\\\"q99\\\"]), np.array(action_norm_stats[\\\"q01\\\"])\\n        else:\\n            raise ValueError(\\\"Unsupported action/proprio normalization type detected!\\\")\\n\\n        actions = np.where(\\n            mask,\\n            0.5 * (normalized_actions + 1) * (action_high - action_low + 1e-8) + action_low,\\n            normalized_actions,\\n        )\\n\\n        return actions\\n\\n    def _run_diffusion_prediction(\\n        self,\\n        input_embeddings,\\n        all_actions_mask,\\n        noise,\\n        action_head,\\n        projected_patch_embeddings,\\n        labels,\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.43}}"}

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"    def _regression_or_discrete_prediction(\\n        self,\\n        input_embeddings,\\n        all_actions_mask,\\n        projected_patch_embeddings,\\n        attention_mask,\\n        labels,\\n        NUM_PATCHES,\\n        NUM_PROMPT_TOKENS,\\n        action_head=None,\\n    ):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Run L1 regression-based continuous action prediction or discrete action tokens prediction.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        # Zero out action token embeddings\\n        all_actions_mask = all_actions_mask.unsqueeze(-1)  # (B, seq_len, 1)\\n        input_embeddings = input_embeddings * ~all_actions_mask\\n\\n        # Build multimodal embeddings and attention mask\\n        multimodal_embeddings, multimodal_attention_mask = self._build_multimodal_attention(\\n            input_embeddings, projected_patch_embeddings, attention_mask\\n        )\\n\\n        # Forward pass through language model\\n        language_model_output = self.language_model(\\n            input_ids=None,\\n            attention_mask=multimodal_attention_mask,\\n            position_ids=None,\\n            past_key_values=None,\\n            inputs_embeds=multimodal_embeddings,\\n            labels=None,\\n            use_cache=None,\\n            output_attentions=False,\\n            output_hidden_states=True,\\n            return_dict=True,\\n        )\\n\\n        # Extract hidden states for action tokens\\n        last_hidden_states = language_model_output.hidden_states[-1]  # (B, seq_len, D)\\n        actions_hidden_states = last_hidden_states[\\n            :,\\n            NUM_PATCHES + NUM_PROMPT_TOKENS : NUM_PATCHES + NUM_PROMPT_TOKENS + ACTION_DIM * NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK,\\n            :,\\n        ]  # (B, act_chunk_len, D)\\n\\n        # Handle different prediction methods\\n        if action_head is not None:\\n            # L1 regression prediction\\n            normalized_actions = action_head.predict_action(actions_hidden_states)\\n            normalized_actions = normalized_actions.reshape(NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK, ACTION_DIM)\\n            normalized_actions = normalized_actions.float().cpu().detach().numpy()\\n        else:\\n            # Discrete token-based prediction\\n            predicted_action_token_ids = (\\n                language_model_output.logits[\\n                    :,\\n                    NUM_PATCHES + NUM_PROMPT_TOKENS : NUM_PATCHES + NUM_PROMPT_TOKENS + ACTION_DIM * NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK,\\n                ]\\n                .argmax(dim=2)\\n                .cpu()\\n                .numpy()\\n            )\\n            discretized_actions = self.vocab_size - predicted_action_token_ids\\n            discretized_actions = np.clip(discretized_actions - 1, a_min=0, a_max=self.bin_centers.shape[0] - 1)\\n            normalized_actions = self.bin_centers[discretized_actions]\\n            normalized_actions = normalized_actions.reshape(NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK, ACTION_DIM)\\n\\n        return normalized_actions, actions_hidden_states\\n\\n    def predict_action(\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.52}}"}
```

```

So during training:
1. `last_hidden_states` is the full multimodal sequence: [BOS | patches | text_tokens | action_tokens | stop_token]
2. `text_hidden_states = last_hidden_states[:, num_patches:-1]` — this extracts everything after the patches and before the last token (stop).


3. The sequence layout is: index 0 is BOS, indices 1 through num_patches are the patch embeddings, and everything from index num_patches+1 onward contains the text and action tokens plus the stop token.

4. When slicing with `last_hidden_states[:, num_patches:-1]`, I'm getting from index num_patches (the last patch) through the second-to-last position, which means I'm including that final patch embedding along with all the text and action tokens but excluding the stop token.

5. This still feels off—I need to look at the actual training code to see exactly how `num_patches` is calculated and how the multimodal sequence is constructed.

1. **Off-by-one fix was WRONG**: The original `a_start = n_patches + n_prompt` was correct. My fix to `1 + n_patches + n_prompt` broke it. Need to revert.

2. **Missing STOP token**: The official code appends a STOP token (token_id=2, `&lt;/s>`) after the action placeholder tokens. Our eval script does NOT add this token. During training, the model always sees this STOP token, so it affects the causal attention pattern and hidden states. The training extracts `last_hidden_states[:, num_patches:-1]` which uses `:-1` to exclude the STOP token.


3. **Placeholder token value**: The official code uses ones while ours uses zeros, but since both get zeroed out before the LLM forward pass, this shouldn't affect inference. The label differences only matter during training loss computation, not during inference.

Now I need to fix the eval script by reverting the off-by-one change and adding the missing STOP token. I need to also check the attention mask handling—the official code extends it for both action and stop tokens, but ours only covers the action tokens since we're not adding a stop token yet. Let me go fix the eval script on the server.

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"@torch.inference_mode()\\ndef predict_actions_oft(\\n    model, processor, img_pil, wrist_pil, task_desc,\\n    proprio_normed, action_head, proprio_proj, unnorm_key, device,\\n    use_proprio=True, use_wrist=True,\\n):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"OFT action prediction: single forward pass + L1 regression action head.\\n\\n    Returns: np.ndarray (NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK, ACTION_DIM) = (8, 7)\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    prompt = f\\\"In: What action should the robot take to {task_desc.lower()}?\\\\nOut:\\\"\\n\\n    # Tokenize prompt\\n    inputs = processor(prompt, img_pil).to(device, dtype=torch.bfloat16)\\n    input_ids = inputs[\\\"input_ids\\\"]\\n    attention_mask = inputs[\\\"attention_mask\\\"]\\n    primary_pixels = inputs[\\\"pixel_values\\\"]\\n\\n    # Ensure trailing empty token (29871) to match training\\n    if input_ids[0, -1].item() != 29871:\\n        input_ids = torch.cat(\\n            [input_ids, torch.tensor([[29871]], device=device, dtype=input_ids.dtype)],\\n            dim=1,\\n        )\\n        attention_mask = torch.cat(\\n            [attention_mask, torch.ones(1, 1, device=device, dtype=attention_mask.dtype)],\\n            dim=1,\\n        )\\n\\n    n_prompt = input_ids.shape[-1] - 1\\n    n_act_tokens = ACTION_DIM * NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK  # 56\\n\\n    # Extend with action placeholder tokens\\n    input_ids_ext = torch.cat(\\n        [input_ids, torch.zeros(1, n_act_tokens, device=device, dtype=input_ids.dtype)],\\n        dim=1,\\n    )\\n    attn_ext = torch.cat(\\n        [attention_mask, torch.ones(1, n_act_tokens, device=device, dtype=attention_mask.dtype)],\\n        dim=1,\\n    )\\n\\n    # Embed and zero-out action positions\\n    input_embeds = model.get_input_embeddings()(input_ids_ext)\\n    input_embeds[:, -n_act_tokens:, :] = 0\\n\\n    # Vision features\\n    patches_primary = model.vision_backbone(primary_pixels)\\n\\n    if use_wrist and wrist_pil is not None:\\n        wrist_inputs = processor(prompt, wrist_pil).to(device, dtype=torch.bfloat16)\\n        patches_wrist = model.vision_backbone(wrist_inputs[\\\"pixel_values\\\"])\\n        all_patches = torch.cat([patches_primary, patches_wrist], dim=1)\\n    else:\\n        all_patches = patches_primary\\n\\n    projected = model.projector(all_patches)\\n\\n    # Proprio token\\n    if use_proprio and proprio_proj is not None and proprio_normed is not None:\\n        proprio_t = torch.tensor(\\n            proprio_normed, dtype=projected.dtype, device=device\\n        )\\n        proprio_emb = proprio_proj(proprio_t.unsqueeze(0)).unsqueeze(1)\\n        projected = torch.cat([projected, proprio_emb], dim=1)\\n\\n    n_patches = projected.shape[1]\\n\\n    # Build multimodal sequence: [first_token | patches | rest_tokens]\\n    multimodal = torch.cat(\\n        [input_embeds[:, :1, :], projected, input_embeds[:, 1:, :]],\\n        dim=1,\\n    )\\n    patch_attn = torch.ones(1, n_patches, device=device, dtype=attn_ext.dtype)\\n    multimodal_attn = torch.cat(\\n        [attn_ext[:, :1], patch_attn, attn_ext[:, 1:]],\\n        dim=1,\\n    )\\n\\n    # LLM forward\\n    outputs = model.language_model(\\n        inputs_embeds=multimodal,\\n        attention_mask=multimodal_attn,\\n        output_hidden_states=True,\\n        return_dict=True,\\n    )\\n\\n    # Extract action hidden states\\n    last_h = outputs.hidden_states[-1]\\n    a_start = n_patches + n_prompt\\n    a_end = a_start + n_act_tokens\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"Utils for training/fine-tuning scripts.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport torch\\n\\nfrom prismatic.vla.constants import ACTION_DIM, ACTION_TOKEN_BEGIN_IDX, IGNORE_INDEX\\n\\n\\ndef get_current_action_mask(token_ids):\\n    # Create a tensor marking positions of IGNORE_INDEX\\n    newline_positions = token_ids != IGNORE_INDEX\\n\\n    # Calculate cumulative sum to identify regions between newlines\\n    cumsum = torch.cumsum(newline_positions, dim=1)\\n\\n    # Create the mask\\n    mask = (1 <= cumsum) & (cumsum <= ACTION_DIM)\\n\\n    # Extract the action part only\\n    action_tokens_only_mask = token_ids > ACTION_TOKEN_BEGIN_IDX\\n    mask = action_tokens_only_mask * mask\\n\\n    return mask\\n\\n\\ndef get_next_actions_mask(token_ids):\\n    # Create a tensor marking positions of IGNORE_INDEX\\n    newline_positions = token_ids != IGNORE_INDEX\\n\\n    # Calculate cumulative sum to identify regions between newlines\\n    cumsum = torch.cumsum(newline_positions, dim=1)\\n\\n    # Create the mask\\n    mask = cumsum > ACTION_DIM\\n\\n    # Extract the action part only\\n    action_tokens_only_mask = token_ids > ACTION_TOKEN_BEGIN_IDX\\n    mask = action_tokens_only_mask * mask\\n\\n    return mask\\n\\n\\ndef compute_token_accuracy(predicted_token_ids, ground_truth_token_ids, mask):\\n    correct_preds = (predicted_token_ids == ground_truth_token_ids) & mask\\n    accuracy = correct_preds.sum().float() / mask.sum().float()\\n    return accuracy\\n\\n\\ndef compute_actions_l1_loss(action_tokenizer, predicted_token_ids, ground_truth_token_ids, mask):\\n    pred_continuous_actions = torch.tensor(\\n        action_tokenizer.decode_token_ids_to_actions(predicted_token_ids[mask].cpu().numpy())\\n    )\\n    true_continuous_actions = torch.tensor(\\n        action_tokenizer.decode_token_ids_to_actions(ground_truth_token_ids[mask].cpu().numpy())\\n    )\\n    l1_loss = torch.nn.functional.l1_loss(pred_continuous_actions, true_continuous_actions)\\n    return l1_loss\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
I see something crucial! The official code uses `AutoModelForVision2Seq.from_pretrained()`, but our code uses `AutoModelForImageTextToText.from_pretrained()`.

But also, critically, the official code does:
```python
vla.vision_backbone.set_num_images_in_input(cfg.num_images_in_input)
```

This sets the number of images to 2 (for both primary + wrist camera). This is important because `get_num_patches()` is multiplied by `get_num_images_in_input()`.

But in our eval script, do we call `set_num_images_in_input`? Let me check.


I need to verify whether `AutoModelForVision2Seq` and `AutoModelForImageTextToText` are equivalent — in newer transformers versions they should be the same class, just renamed — and whether `trust_remote_code=True` is being used to load the custom model classes from the checkpoint.

The critical issue is whether our eval script calls `set_num_images_in_input(2)`, because if `get_num_images_in_input()` defaults to 1 instead of 2, the `NUM_PATCHES` calculation would be halved and break the action hidden state extraction. But our approach of computing `n_patches` directly from `projected.shape[1]` should give us the correct value regardless, since we're measuring the actual projected tokens including all modalities.

The official `predict_action` method would fail without the correct `set_num_images_in_input` call, but we're not using that — we're computing everything manually. So that shouldn't be the problem.

Let me check whether the model loading is consistent. The official `get_vla` uses `AutoModelForVision2Seq`, which maps to `OpenVLAForActionPrediction`, but our code uses `AutoModelForImageTextToText`. I need to verify if these load different model architectures or if they're equivalent.
````
